People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

Hello, Doctor. Well, I came in today because... I've been struggling with something at work lately. I'm a secondary school teacher, and recently, I just can't shake this feeling that certain staff emails and the morning announcements are actually coded messages about me. 

My colleagues have been very gentle about it, and they've sat down and shown me that these emails are just standard memos sent to everyone on staff, but... I just can't seem to let the feeling go. It's really starting to bother me, so I thought I should come in and talk to someone about it.

I have been hearing something. Sometimes, when it's really quiet... like when I'm at home by myself, or right after all the students leave the classroom for the day... I hear a woman's voice. \n\nIt's usually just my name, or maybe a short little comment about whatever I happen to be doing right then. But it's so clear that I actually answer back out loud, just automatically, before I even realize that I'm completely alone in the room.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having these experiences with the coded messages and hearing the voice?\n\nPatient: Well, let's see... I think it's been about four months now. Yeah, around four months since I first started worrying about the messages and hearing the voice.\n\nDoctor: Have these experiences been going on continuously for at least six months?\n\nPatient: Well, the specific things—the messages and the voice—have only been happening for about four months. But if you mean the broader situation... yes, it's been more than six months. \n\nThis overall change in how I think, how I'm handling my work, and just connecting with people... that's been going on for longer. I'd say that all started right around the time my youngest left for college, which was about a year and two months ago.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods lasting at least a week when your mood was unusually elevated or irritable, with much more energy than usual?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't experienced anything like that. I definitely haven't had any bursts of energy or felt unusually high... or overly irritable for days on end. Honestly, with everything going on, I've mostly just been trying to keep up with my classes. So, no, my energy hasn't been higher than normal.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there.   },

    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, yes, I suppose I have. I mean, I still care about my students, and my friends, of course. But... for many months now, it just feels like the warmth and the drive have sort of drained out of me. 

I'm just not as expressive or engaged. That's why I've let things like the book club, the theater planning, and even just simple phone calls slide. I just don't initiate anything anymore unless someone really pushes me to do it. It's just... hard to muster up the energy to react or be involved the way I used to be.

I used to go to book club, the theater, different neighborhood events... but I just haven't been keeping up with any of that lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed or lost interest or pleasure in things most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really had anything like that. I mean, I definitely haven't felt depressed or down for weeks at a time. And I haven't lost interest in things because of my mood... it's just that I haven't been going to my usual activities because of everything else that's been happening. But no, I haven't had a two-week period of feeling depressed like you described.\n\nDoctor: Have you been using any alcohol, recreational drugs, or new medications around the time these symptoms started?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. I don't really drink, maybe just a glass of wine at a holiday dinner, but that's it. And I definitely don't use any recreational drugs. As for medications, I haven't started anything new recently... just my usual vitamins. So, no, nothing out of the ordinary there.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed becoming noticeably less emotional or expressive than you used to be?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, I suppose I have. I mean, I still care about my students, and my friends, of course. But... for many months now, it just feels like the warmth and the drive have sort of drained out of me. \n\nI'm just not as expressive or engaged. That's why I've let things like the book club, the theater planning, and even just simple phone calls slide. I just don't initiate anything anymore unless someone really pushes me to do it. It's just... hard to muster up the energy to react or be involved the way I used to be.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that someone else was controlling your thoughts, feelings, or actions?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't ever felt anything like that. My thoughts and my actions are my own. I mean... even though I don't have the energy to do much these days, it's definitely still me making the choice to step back from things. I've never felt like someone else was pulling the strings or controlling my mind, no.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed or lost interest or pleasure in things nearly every day?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I've felt depressed. I know I just mentioned losing interest in my book club and the theater planning, but... it's not because I feel sad or hopeless. It doesn't really feel like depression. It's more just that lack of energy and warmth I was talking about. I don't sit around feeling down or crying or anything like that. So, no, I haven't had a period like you're describing.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I think it's been about four months now. Yeah, around four months since I first started worrying about the messages and hearing the voice.\n\nDoctor: Have these experiences been going on continuously for at least six months?\n\nPatient: Well, the specific things—the messages and the voice—have only been happening for about four months. But if you mean the broader situation... yes, it's been more than six months. \n\nThis overall change in how I think, how I'm handling my work, and just connecting with people... that's been going on for longer. I'd say that all started right around the time my youngest left for college, which was about a year and two months ago.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods lasting at least a week when your mood was unusually elevated or irritable, with much more energy than usual?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't experienced anything like that. I definitely haven't had any bursts of energy or felt unusually high... or overly irritable for days on end. Honestly, with everything going on, I've mostly just been trying to keep up with my classes. So, no, my energy hasn't been higher than normal.\n\nDoctor: Have there been any times when the messages or the voice happened only while you were also feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean... I haven't really noticed feeling depressed. The messages and the voice just happen on their own, regardless of how I'm feeling. It doesn't seem to be tied to my mood or feeling down at all.\n\nDoctor: Have these experiences made it harder for you to function at work or in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Yes, it definitely has. I mean, I’m still teaching, but... I've had to rely on my coworkers a lot more. I've actually needed them to step in and cover parts of my project lessons, and they've really had to help me just get through our planning meetings. \n\nAnd outside of work, in my personal life, I've just stopped being reliable. I used to go to book club, the theater, different neighborhood events... but I just haven't been keeping up with any of that lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed or lost interest or pleasure in things most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really had anything like that. I mean, I definitely haven't felt depressed or down for weeks at a time. And I haven't lost interest in things because of my mood... it's just that I haven't been going to my usual activities because of everything else that's been happening. But no, I haven't had a two-week period of feeling depressed like you described.\n\nDoctor: Have you been using any alcohol, recreational drugs, or new medications around the time these symptoms started?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. I don't really drink, maybe just a glass of wine at a holiday dinner, but that's it. And I definitely don't use any recreational drugs. As for medications, I haven't started anything new recently... just my usual vitamins. So, no, nothing out of the ordinary there.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed becoming noticeably less emotional or expressive than you used to be?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, I suppose I have. I mean, I still care about my students, and my friends, of course. But... for many months now, it just feels like the warmth and the drive have sort of drained out of me. \n\nI'm just not as expressive or engaged. That's why I've let things like the book club, the theater planning, and even just simple phone calls slide. I just don't initiate anything anymore unless someone really pushes me to do it. It's just... hard to muster up the energy to react or be involved the way I used to be.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that someone else was controlling your thoughts, feelings, or actions?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't ever felt anything like that. My thoughts and my actions are my own. I mean... even though I don't have the energy to do much these days, it's definitely still me making the choice to step back from things. I've never felt like someone else was pulling the strings or controlling my mind, no.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed or lost interest or pleasure in things nearly every day?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I've felt depressed. I know I just mentioned losing interest in my book club and the theater planning, but... it's not because I feel sad or hopeless. It doesn't really feel like depression. It's more just that lack of energy and warmth I was talking about. I don't sit around feeling down or crying or anything like that. So, no, I haven't had a period like you're describing.\n\nDoctor: Have the unusual messages and the voice been present for a total of six months or more?\n\nPatient: No, not quite that long. I'd say... well, the worries about the coded messages, and that voice I sometimes answer, they've been happening for about four months now. So it hasn't been six months yet.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing the woman’s voice even when there’s clearly no one there?\n\nPatient: Yes, that's right. It usually happens when it's really quiet, like when I'm alone in my house, or... or right after the students leave and the classroom is empty. I'll just hear this woman's voice. Sometimes she just says my name, or she might make a little comment about whatever I'm doing at the moment. It sounds so clear that I actually answer her out loud... and then I look around and realize there's no one else in the room.\n\nDoctor: Have the coded messages and the voice ever only happened during times when you were feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Well, like I said before, I haven't really felt depressed. So... no, the messages and the voice haven't been connected to anything like that. I'll start rearranging markers or papers on the desk in a very particular pattern. \n\nAnd with my speech... sometimes I’ll just repeat a phrase someone else just said instead of responding to them normally. It’s quite embarrassing, honestly, because it completely interrupts what I’m supposed to be doing.\n\nDoctor: Have the unusual messages and the voice been present for a total of six months or more?\n\nPatient: No, it hasn't been six months. I used to go to book club, the theater, different neighborhood events... but I just haven't been keeping up with any of that lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed or lost interest or pleasure in things most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really had anything like that. I mean, I definitely haven't felt depressed or down for weeks at a time. And I haven't lost interest in things because of my mood... it's just that I haven't been going to my usual activities because of everything else that's been happening. But no, I haven't had a two-week period of feeling depressed like you described.\n\nDoctor: Have you been using any alcohol, recreational drugs, or new medications around the time these symptoms started?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. I don't really drink, maybe just a glass of wine at a holiday dinner, but that's it. And I definitely don't use any recreational drugs. As for medications, I haven't started anything new recently... just my usual vitamins. So, no, nothing out of the ordinary there.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed becoming noticeably less emotional or expressive than you used to be?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, I suppose I have. I mean, I still care about my students, and my friends, of course. But... for many months now, it just feels like the warmth and the drive have sort of drained out of me. \n\nI'm just not as expressive or engaged. That's why I've let things like the book club, the theater planning, and even just simple phone calls slide. I just don't initiate anything anymore unless someone really pushes me to do it. It's just... hard to muster up the energy to react or be involved the way I used to be.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that someone else was controlling your thoughts, feelings, or actions?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't ever felt anything like that. My thoughts and my actions are my own. I mean... even though I don't have the energy to do much these days, it's definitely still me making the choice to step back from things. I've never felt like someone else was pulling the strings or controlling my mind, no.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed or lost interest or pleasure in things nearly every day?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I've felt depressed. It's more just that lack of energy and warmth I was talking about. I don't sit around feeling down or crying or anything like that. So, no, I haven't had a period like you're describing.\n\nDoctor: Have the unusual messages and the voice been present for a total of six months or more?\n\nPatient: No, not quite that long. I'd say... well, the worries about the coded messages, and that voice I sometimes answer, they've been happening for about four months now. So it hasn't been six months yet.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing the woman’s voice even when there’s clearly no one there?\n\nPatient: Yes, that's right. It usually happens when it's really quiet, like when I'm alone in my house, or... or right after the students leave and the classroom is empty. I'll just hear this woman's voice. Sometimes she just says my name, or she might make a little comment about whatever I'm doing at the moment. So... no, the messages and the voice haven't been connected to anything like that. They just happen on their own, regardless of how my mood is.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any periods when your speech or behavior became noticeably disorganized or hard for other people to follow?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, I have noticed some things like that.
